# Third wave — 24 October 2026

## 2026-10-27 — Inspection published; four full-film releases in progress

[Why Your Bus Wait Is Longer Than You Expect](https://youtu.be/ELxgByPW13s) is public. Native 4K60, English captions and YouTube checks verified. [Release receipt](../../../narration_lab/third-wave-20261024/root/five-film-release-20261027/inspection_paradox/publication-receipt.json) retains exact master identities and listening limits. All four remaining films have complete source-preserving narration assemblies. Full native timelines, scoring, final rendering and publication are next; Shamir has a narrow highlight-duration repair under qualification.

## 2026-10-27 — Finish and publish all five films

The owner directly instructed: “dude just finish the damned videos and publish them”. Publication is now authorized. The active path is [full-film delivery](../../../narration_lab/third-wave-20261024/root/five-film-release-20261027/OWNER-INSTRUCTION.md): Inspection first, followed by German Tank, Shamir, Crofton and Parrondo. This supersedes the historical no-publication and chapter-by-chapter-only ordering below. Inspection’s existing complete71-unit take plan is selected for production; the earlier66-unit plan and all audio remain preserved. Native 4K60 timeline/score/render/master work is underway. Selection and publication do not assert listening acceptance.

## Owner direction: teach the intuition before the machinery

The owner explicitly asks us to err toward longer films with more motivation, background, setting the stage and explanation of the point. Twenty minutes or more is welcome when it earns understanding. There is no first-minute derivation deadline. The former 85-second chapter estimate is now advisory in the actual storyboard checker and AGENTS.md; it must not cause useful explanations to be cut or rushed. Six CLI tests and all five current read-only storyboard checks pass ([repair evidence](../../../narration_lab/third-wave-20261024/root/tend-0853/duration-repair.md)). Start with a concrete situation, show why a sensible first answer fails or remains incomplete, let the viewer make a prediction, and only then develop the mathematical tool that resolves it. Keep returning to the same example as the explanation deepens. Added time should resolve a real prerequisite, misconception or consequence, with time to inspect the picture.

FrankenManim should carry the reasoning. Preserve an object's identity when it moves from a physical example into a diagram; couple geometry, counts and plots to the same computed model; show controlled changes and meaningful counterexamples; let transformations reveal where each term comes from. Keep labels and color roles stable. Pause for the particular observation or prediction being requested, then visibly resolve it. Use close-ups, coordinated views and symbolic transformations where they make a relationship easier to see. Capabilities must be verified against the pinned native source; a draft storyboard is not a rendered or reviewed scene.

| Film | Motivating question and prerequisite | Central visual experiment and return |
|---|---|---|
| Inspection paradox | Why does the service's average gap differ from my experience at the stop? Distinguish sampling a bus from sampling a time before averages or variance. | Preserve the same timetable while arrival dots become a visible measure of exposure to each gap. Stretch one gap, compare bus weighting with time weighting, then trace waiting triangles and their areas. Return to the original commuter and show when timetable-aware arrival changes the model. |
| Serial numbers | What could five box numbers reveal about a hidden production count, and why does this matter to someone estimating scarcity? Establish the known lower bound and the unseen tail before averaging gaps. | Keep the five boxes as they line up on a number strip. Reveal unseen slots one gap at a time. Shrink to a toy population where every handful can be seen, then move the same gaps through possible positions to earn the correction. Compare repeated estimates with the unknown true count; show the biased-shop failure using the same objects. |
| Shamir secret sharing | Why are complete copies and ordinary cut-up pieces both unsatisfactory for a group that needs some members, but not everyone, to open a secret? Define a share and the desired threshold before curves or modular arithmetic. | Keep the vault, holders and labelled shares in view as the mathematical representation appears. With too few shares, reveal all possible secrets still compatible with what is seen; adding one share eliminates alternatives. Teach wraparound arithmetic with discrete steps before a prime-field construction. Distinguish geometric intuition from the finite-field security proof and show a failed modulus as a counterexample. |
| Crofton | How could we measure an awkward outline without tracing every bend? Define a crossing and the sampling choices before the integral formula. | Use one curve throughout. Translate and rotate the line grid, highlight actual intersections, and update the count from the same geometry. Straight segments first reveal orientation bias; multiple directions and translations explain why averaging works. Connect local projected contributions to the whole curve and show the finite-sample error. |
| Parrondo | Why should switching between losing games ever help, and what hidden state changes when we switch? Define the rules and remainder with an actual player's moves before stationary weights. | Track one player, then a crowd on the same three-state diagram. Animate conserved flows and occupation weights alongside gains from the same model. Compare A, B and the random mixture; isolate the population shift that changes B's contribution. Return to the opening puzzle with a switching pattern that fails, so the claim's limits are memorable. |

Apply this direction inside the existing scripts and storyboards, with specific visual actions and authored thinking pauses. Finish with a new small prediction the viewer can solve using the picture, then explain the answer. The table is direction for implementation and review, not evidence that those scenes already exist.
